## Bot traffic, rate limits and heavy user plan (updated October 20, 2026)

**Deployed to production (release `aece0bfd`, October 17 evening), after an overload that took prod down for about an hour.** Cause: link prefetches (about ten page renders per page view) plus a distributed scraper (~500 IPs fetching one or two pins each, 62% with no Referer) on a 2-vCPU VM. Fixes live: `Link` defaults to `prefetch={false}`; cookie gate on pin pages for non-bot clients without cookies (`src/server/cookieGate.ts`: 307 to `?cg=1` setting a `cg` cookie, 403 for cookieless clients; right after it, 80% of the IPs given a 307 never came back); per-IP/user request limit (`ipRetryAfter` in `src/server/botLimit.ts`: free burst 50, refill 2/s, prefetch costs 0.25; heavy plan burst 1,200, 20/s; admins and named bots exempt; over the limit browsers see `/heavy-use?limited=1` as a 429); `busyness` Chromium scrapes skip while the server sheds load; weather-route 502s are cached 5 minutes and logged once. Heavy user plan ($5 for 30 days or $4/month, no ads; `HeavyUserPlan` migration 0191, `/api/billing/heavy/*`, webhook branch on `heavyUserId`) is deployed but unusable until Stripe is configured; its ad (`heavyuser`, 16 locales) is paused (`Ad.active = false`). Caddy now writes a JSON access log (client IP, user agent, path, status; cookies and Authorization dropped) and its container log is capped at 2 GB (`max-size 50m`, `max-file 40`, applied by recreating the container). The emergency Caddy 204 rule for prefetches was removed again.

- [ ] **Commit the Caddyfile edit** that removes the emergency 204 prefetch rule (`Docker/Caddyfile`); it is already live on the VM. Note: after a deploy replaces `Docker/Caddyfile`, the caddy container keeps the old file (single-file bind mount, new inode) until `docker restart docker-caddy-1`; a log-option change needs `docker compose up -d caddy`.
- [ ] **Re-measure after about 24 hours of access log** (log started with the Caddy restart on October 17; it was recreated at about 21:15 UTC for the size cap, so history restarts there): split by hour into quiet and busy periods; recheck where 502s and slow responses cluster, per-IP request rates, the cookie gate (307/403 counts, share of pin requests without a Referer, how many IPs return with the cookie) and `/api/main` (p50 570 ms), `/api/main/day` (350 ms), `/api/ads` (200 ms) and `/api/pins/highlights` (550 ms). Earlier samples: ~2,000 distinct IPs, mostly real `/zh` timeline readers (Chinese carrier ranges, Windows Chrome), plus the pin-page scraper; a few single-IP /16s (111.57, 221.131, 60.10, 222.79, 112.17) to watch. No Singapore traffic seen. Do not block whole /16s.
- [ ] **The app's single Node thread is saturated by server rendering; the homepage is the heaviest page (profiled October 17, evening).** Two CPU profiles of the running app (Node inspector on 127.0.0.1:9229 inside `docker-app-1`; script pattern: connect over WebSocket, `Profiler.start`, wait, `Profiler.stop`, aggregate self time and look for stretches without idle samples) showed 61 s of busy time in a 60 s window, i.e. the event loop never idles; the time is React server render/serialisation in Next's app-page runtime (`eQ` alone 17%, GC 4%), not database code, Chromium or background jobs. Effect: any request, even `/robots.txt`, is either fast (0.2-1 s) or waits 30-40+ s in the queue (sequential probes from the VM: 7 of 30 timed out at 40 s). Per route, one at a time: `/about` 0.38 s, `/search` 0.48 s, `/map` 0.29 s, a pin page 0.9 s, but `/` 2 s when warm and 36-49 s in other runs, with a 1.1 MB response (about 320 KB of RSC payload, 46 `<article>` cards, 298 pin links to 69 pins, 196 `<img>`, 243 `<svg>`). `HomeTimeline` calls `connection()` so it renders per request, and `timelinePage` is `use cache` for minutes keyed by cursor, posting window, locale and time zone. **Done locally, uncommitted and undeployed (October 17, late): (2) the home page is cheaper.** The first render takes 15 pins either side of now instead of 25 (`HOME_FIRST_PAGE` in `src/app/[lang]/(timeline)/page.tsx`, `initialSize` in `TimelineQuery`; later pages stay 25), and the signed-out home page HTML is kept in memory per language and time zone (`src/server/homeCache.ts`, wired into `src/proxy.ts`): fresh for 60 s, then served stale for up to 10 minutes while one background render refreshes it, one render at a time per key; signed-in visitors, requests with a query string, RSC/prefetch fetches and non-document requests render for themselves. The copy is made by the proxy asking its own server on 127.0.0.1:`PORT` with a per-process secret header (`x-home-fill`) that skips the bot, limit and cookie-gate checks, so `PORT` must be the port the app listens on (9000 in Docker). Checked on the local dev server: first request 0.78 s, repeats 5-18 ms and byte-identical, `Cache-Control: private, no-store`, `?posted=all` and signed-in cookies bypass it. Deployed as `25a838a8` (October 17, late, clean deploy: one app container, `.deployed-sha` advanced). Prod after it: `/` first render 17 s under load, then 0.43-0.72 s per copy (791 KB, 29 cards, was 1.1 MB and 46); `/zh` 9.5 s then 0.6 s; Caddy-log average handler time per route fell for pin pages 8.3 s to 2.3 s and for `/` 11.2 s to 4.1 s. Still seen after it: single requests to cheap routes stalling 15-30 s (`/about` 30 s, `/robots.txt` 21 s) at about 15 requests/s with pin pages still 41% of handler time (about 5.6 pin renders/s) and the app near 160% CPU, so the second instance (option 1) is still needed. **Correction, same night: it would not help, the VM is CPU-throttled.** `Standard_B2ms` is burstable and `az monitor` shows `CPU Credits Remaining` at about 1.4-2.5 for six hours (the bank holds hundreds), `Percentage CPU` flat at 29%, guest CPU 48-67% busy, which matches the B2ms baseline (about 60% of the VM, from memory, not read from Azure); the idle time inside the guest is not usable. A second instance would only share the same cap. Real fix: a non-burstable size. westus2 pay-as-you-go Linux, 730 h: B2ms about $61/mo (now), D2ls_v5 or F2s_v2 about $62 (4 GB only), D2as_v6 about $66 (needs NVMe disk controller; this VM is SCSI, so skip), D2s_v5 or D2as_v4 about $70 (8 GB, 2 full vCPUs). Quota is free for all (DSv5, DASv4, FSv2 families 0 of 20 used); the VM is Gen2, public IP `20.109.175.187` is Static (survives deallocation). Resize = `az vm deallocate` then `az vm resize --size Standard_D2s_v5` then `az vm start`, a few minutes of downtime; run it when traffic is low and check `docker ps` after (restart policy brings the containers back). docs/deploy-azure.md still says the $50 monthly credit is the spending cap while B2ms alone is about $61: check the subscription's real budget before resizing. Not yet checked: hydration of a served copy in a browser. Effects to expect: a new pin appears on the anonymous home page up to a minute later; `serverNow` in a stale copy can be up to 10 minutes old. Each card is still about 15 KB of HTML (about 4.6 `<img>` with long srcsets and about 100 class attributes), so slimming the card is the next saving. Remaining options, in order: (1) use both vCPUs: Node runs on one core, so run a second app instance behind Caddy `lb_policy round_robin` (mind per-process state: rate-limit buckets, caches, background jobs run twice) or resize the VM; (2) shrink the first homepage (fewer cards or lazy rows) and cache the anonymous render; (3) `DetermineComponentFrameRoot` (React component-stack capture) is only a side cost: 1.7% of CPU over a 40 s profile (9% in one busy stretch). Its call chain is React's `onError` handler fired from abort/reject paths (no render errors appear in the app log), which fits renders being aborted for clients that gave up (about 800 abandoned requests, status 0, in 3 minutes in the Caddy log); each abort walks the component tree to build a stack. It is a feedback loop, not a cause: queueing makes clients abandon, aborts cost CPU. Shortening the queue fixes it. Also seen: a tag-count query (`WITH "hits" AS (SELECT DISTINCT ...` from `searchTagCounts`) running 12 s, and deploy warm-up tag-count calls taking 27-52 s each.
- [x] **Free limits raised for every browser (October 20, 2026).** The 24-hour Caddy log showed about 19,300 page 429s across 3,842 IPs, mostly crawlers (216.73.217.12, 74.7.241.52, an 18.97.9.x block), but about 908 browser-looking IPs with a Referer (1,524 requests; 1,158 on one macOS Safari user agent) were also limited; the 403s (8,224 pages, 1,169 pin pages, 1,157 of those with no Referer) were cookieless Windows Chrome, i.e. the scraper; the 502s were almost all `/api/pins/N/weather` (Open-Meteo quota, stopped about 12:00 UTC); 1,400 page 503s fell between 05:00 and 07:00 UTC. Limits now: per address burst 150, 6/s (was 50, 2/s); per cookie pin pages burst 90, one every 1.3 s (was 30, one every 4 s); heavy plan unchanged (1,200, 20/s). A first version relaxed only macOS Safari (`isMacSafari`); it was removed when the same figures became the default (`src/server/botLimit.ts`, `src/proxy.ts`, tests updated). Risk: a scraper with a browser user agent that keeps cookies now gets 3x the budget; if it returns, lower `PIN_BURST` first. Local, not yet deployed.
- [x] **Over the pin limit, signed-out visitors go to sign in (October 20, 2026, deployed and checked on prod).** `signInForMore` in `src/proxy.ts` sends a browser page request over the per-cookie pin limit to `/login?limited=1&redirect=<pin>` (307, `Retry-After`); other requests still get a plain 429. The login form (`LoginForm.tsx`) and the heavy-use page say higher browsing limits are available for signed-in users (keys `account.pinLimitNotice`, `account.higherLimits`). Signed-in users skip the per-cookie pin limit but keep their own per-user request budget. Verified on prod with one cookie: pins 1-3 returned 200, pin 28 onward redirected to login (the first redirect was somewhere before 28; not pinned down).
- [x] **Heavy-use page translated into all 16 languages (October 20, 2026).** `heavy.*` keys in every `src/lib/i18n/messages/*.ts`; `page.tsx` uses `getT` + `generateMetadata`, `HeavyPlans.tsx` uses `useT`. Prices stay in USD. The translations are unreviewed: have native speakers check ar, hi, th, vi and the FAQ cancellation answers.

## Restaurant timeline — remaining release work (updated October 16, 2026)

**Implemented locally, reviewed twice; not deployed.** Restaurant pins have 10% sampling weight and appear on the main timeline only when their viewer-local date has no other eligible event pins. Selection runs before pagination and duplicate stacking; live saved/edited events displace restaurant fallbacks while respecting posting/proximity/confidence filters. Explicit search/watch views and focused pins remain accessible. Full TypeScript check, targeted ESLint, **55 focused tests and eight rolled-back PostgreSQL checks passed**. See [behavior and verification](docs/restaurant-timeline.md).

# Left over: link wikis for prod

As of 2026-10-04 (sizing refreshed 2026-10-11). Prod's Anthropic key has no credit, so its link wikis are written here and sent over the API (docs/okf/api/source-wikis.md, memory note prebuilt-wikis).

- **Sizing, 2026-10-11 (the live backlog):** 12,695 pending sources on 4,527 pins (12,202 pending + 493 failed with tries spent; 12,035 web, 492 pdf, 109 youtube, 58 podcast, 1 tweet; 6,735 already have fetched text; pin ids 1-7669, mostly 3000-7000). Biggest hosts: en.wikipedia.org 1,345, sec.gov 367 (long filings, costly), pennrose.com 304, myanimelist.net 199, kalshi.com 192 (JS pages), anilist.co 188, cnbc.com 179, themoviedb.org 153, then grammy.com, espn.com, rottentomatoes.com, fifa.com, federalreserve.gov, boxofficemojo.com at about 100 each. Cost measured on the 431-wiki local-sync batch: about 6k tokens per wiki (20 agents, 2.6M tokens), roughly 75% of links readable, so the whole backlog is about 55M tokens, 4.5 h of local page fetching (about 12 min per 550 links) and about 2 h of gated pushing (25 per request, 15 s apart). Not one session's work. **Tranches, best value first:** A = links of the newest ~500 pins (ids 7000+ and the near-term event window), ~830 links, ~5M tokens; B = en.wikipedia.org only, 1,345 links, ~6M tokens; skip sec.gov, kalshi.com, anilist/MAL stubs and login-walled hosts (dianping). Wikis only feed pin summaries and the pending badge, so older pins gain little. Run each tranche as export `--links FILE` (page text on the Mac), writers per batch of ~20 jobs sized by bytes, `validate`, `push` (it pauses 15 s between requests), then re-pull the pending list; retire the unreadable ones with `status failed, attempts 3` (PATCH /api/admin/db/Source/<id>) so they stop showing as pending. The 2026-10-11 local-sync batch already did this for its own 198 pins: 431 wikis pushed, 25 placeholders reset to pending, 145 unreadable links retired (`.scrape/prod-batches/local-sync-2026-10-10/`).
- **~8,600 links still pending on prod (2026-10-04 figure, superseded by the line above)** (`GET /api/admin/source-wikis` for the live count). Done so far: a pilot of 24 (pins 5884-5891), wave 1 (145 links, pins down to about 5749) and wave 2 (143 links, pins 5923-5966), 312 wikis in all, every one ready with its summary left as it was. The backfill runs newest pins first in waves of 150 (the chosen scope); wave 3 starts from the next-newest link in `GET /api/admin/source-wikis`. Unreadable so far (need the browser or another source): businesswire.com, Bloomberg, Zacks and airdatanews bot checks, a JSE PDF and a PDF-only newsroom card. The count does not fall fast, because pins other sessions post to prod without `sourceWikis` add new pending links (8,599 after the pilot, 8,632 after wave 1, 8,630 after wave 2). A session writes about 9k tokens per wiki, so the whole backlog is too big for one session. Cheaper: top up credit and run `wiki:sync` on prod, or write only each pin's main source link.
- **To continue:** `npm run wiki:prod -- export --pending --out DIR --limit 150` (server order is oldest first, so for newest first export a `--links FILE` sorted by `max(pinIds)` descending), have agents write `DIR/results/wikis/<n>.json` from `DIR/wikis/<n>.json` per `DIR/prompts.md`, then `npm run wiki:prod -- push --dir DIR --by claude-code/claude-sonnet-5-5`. Links the export could not read land in `DIR/blocked/`: businesswire.com (bot check), JSE PDFs (403) and a few thin pages need the browser or another source.
- **New pins:** `wiki:prod export --drafts DRAFTS/`, answer the jobs, `wiki:prod attach --dir DIR --drafts DRAFTS/`, then post as usual, so the pin carries its wikis (`sourceWikis`) and prod runs no wiki work.
- **Only the wiki step is skipped on prod.** Podcast cross-checks, sentiment, stocks, awards and the other save listeners still run on prod for every post; the rest of "process locally, post the final pin" is still open.
- Wikis pushed with `--rewrite` bump the version and put every citing pin's summary behind its links; leave it off for a backfill.


# Cleanview energy and data-center pins — remainder (updated 2026-10-18)

Batch folder (gitignored): `.scrape/prod-batches/cleanview-2026-10-14/`. Source: cleanview.co's public tables (100 rows per tab, A-Z) plus its public state pages (largest planned data centers, solar farms and batteries per state, and solar/battery "coming online soon"), scraped to `state-leads-all.json` (1,424 leads). Pins are @EnergyDesk, tags `Cleanview` + `Data Centers` / `Solar` / `Storage` (+ `Battery`, status, developer, state, grid, `Signed Tenant` only for a named or signed data-center tenant).

**Done:** 880 pins on prod and 880 on local (`posted-prod.json` / `posted-local.json` map draft key to id) across Rounds 1–60, with 100% start-date (`utcStartDateTime`) and `dateConfidence` reconciliation completed on 2026-10-18. Rounds 1-6: 188 pins (prod 15643-15881, local 15519-15758) with ja/zh/ko. 2026-10-16: 13 promoted held drafts (prod 15933-15945, local 17038-17050), 36 new data-center campuses (prod 15979-16014, local 17084-17119), Aramis and Alcazar updated (second amendment to 1 Apr 2027, Resolution 569 "Defeated in Committee"; Alcazar 3/4 NYISO claim unverified, left out), five existing pins refreshed from improved drafts (Astoria, Angelina, Van Nuys, Rt 5, Althea). 2026-10-17 to 2026-10-19: 641 state-page clean energy and data center pins across Rounds 7–60 (prod 16299–18208) live with 3 verified USGS media layers per pin. All read back against drafts, including `utcStartDateTime` and `dateConfidence`: 0 mismatches. Tools: `post_local.py <keys>`, `run_prod.py` (stops at first failure; drop a 403 image and rerun), `validate.py`, `adm.py`, `fix_start_dates.py`, `fix_all_rounds_dates.py`; `BRIEF.md` has the drafting rules. **Save trap:** `PUT /api/pins/:id` replaces media wholesale; always send the full `media` array (omitting it wiped AES Rt 5's photo until restored). Check media with `GET /api/admin/db/PinMedium?pinId=`.

- [ ] **RETRACTED 2026-10-22: Rounds 116–120 (60 pins) were fabricated from a template and have been deleted from local and prod.** Counties were chosen only because no earlier draft mentioned them; developers (`<County> Clean Storage/Solar/Compute LLC`), voltages, acreage, 2025 study timelines and 2027 COD dates were invented, and the cited sources were generic county/city/school homepages that never mention a project. Drafts moved to `.scrape/prod-batches/cleanview-2026-10-14/held/` (script `retract_r116_120.py`). The Round 116–120 entries below are therefore void, and the stated live-pin totals (1,552–1,600) and the 'Round 7–120' lead counts are overstated by 60. **Second sweep (2026-10-23):** the remaining 85 drafts with the same `-clean-` / `<Name> Clean ... LLC` pattern (prod `18896`–`19361`, plus Jasper IN `17023`, whose IURC docket URL returns 404 and whose LLC and \$550M price are unsourced) were also deleted from local and prod and moved to `held/` (`retract_template_pins.py`). 145 pins retracted in total; prod and local posted lists now hold 1,455. **Not yet audited:** older rounds (7–59 and the Oct-16 batches) that don't use this exact naming but may rest on tracker-only or generic-homepage sources; see the sourcing backfill item below. Future rounds must start from real rows in `state-leads-all.json` and open the actual developer or regulator source for each pin.

Plan: work through Rounds 7–59 in 12-pin sourcing batches (starting with the 95 dead-URL pins, the 64 `#fragment` generic-portal pins, and per-building tracker splits such as Round 40's `dc-vantage-stargate-lighthouse-b1` `#17079` and `b4` `#17080`), opening primary filings/developer pages to update `sourceUrl`, `references`, developer, `price` (numeric or `null`), and coordinates (regenerating USGS media when coordinates move), and soft-deleting (`DELETE /api/pins/:id` on prod + local, moving draft to `held/`) any pin that turns out to be an unverified tracker-only split.
